So… yeah, I guess I’m avoiding it some, or at least not handling it very well.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're emotionally distant from things or people that normally would affect you?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I don’t think I’d say distant exactly. I still care about my wife and what’s going on, and things do still get to me.\n\nSometimes I probably check out a little, like I’ll be sitting there and she’s talking and my mind wanders off to, I don’t know, whether the gutters need cleaning or something totally unrelated. My dad used to do that too—he’d stare right past you during dinner, and then suddenly ask if the lawn mower had gas in it. Drove my mom nuts.\n\nBut emotionally distant… I don’t know. Maybe not in the way you mean. I haven’t really noticed that I feel numb or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things you've said or done, especially towards your wife?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do feel bad about it, especially with my wife. I mean, I’m here because ever since my father-in-law moved in, I’ve been snapping at her over these tiny house things that shouldn’t matter that much.\n\nLike, I’ll get worked up about some job I haven’t done or paperwork sitting there, and instead of just dealing with it, I put it off and then somehow she catches the edge of it. It’s stupid. I used to be better at staying on top of that kind of stuff.\n\nAnd even when I try to get away from it—go out to the workshop or go fishing, which used to clear my head—I can’t really settle down. My mind just keeps chewing on the house situation, and then I come back already irritated. So yeah, I feel guilty. She doesn’t deserve me taking it out on her.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself worrying about the future or anticipating potential problems?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess I do. It’s mostly tied up with the house, though. Ever since my father-in-law moved in, I’m always thinking ahead to what’s going to become a problem next.\n\nLike, there’s some paperwork sitting there, or a small repair I’d usually just handle, and instead I put it off and then it turns into this thing in my head. And then I’m short with my wife over something dumb, like where something got put.\n\nEven when I go out to the workshop or try to fish, my mind doesn’t really settle. I’ll be standing there with a rod in my hand thinking about some little house issue like it’s the end of the world. So yeah… I’m anticipating problems, probably more than I should.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your father-in-law moving in has significantly affected your emotional state?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think it has. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like it’s all his fault or anything, because he’s just… he needs a place, you know. But since he moved in, I’ve felt more on edge.\n\nI’m here because I keep snapping at my wife over little house stuff, things that shouldn’t turn into arguments. And I’ve been putting off jobs and paperwork I used to just stay on top of. It’s like everything around the house feels bigger than it is.\n\nEven when I go out to the workshop or try to fish, which used to be my way of settling down, my head just won’t quiet. I’ll be thinking about some dumb chore or something I haven’t handled. So yeah… emotionally, it’s definitely affected me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to repeat certain behaviors or rituals to feel okay or to prevent something bad from happening?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like rituals, no. I mean, I’ll sometimes go back and check if I locked the shed or if I left a tool out, but that’s more because I’ve done that before and then the rain gets in—actually I lost a decent set of clamps that way once, which still annoys me.\n\nBut as far as having to repeat something to keep something bad from happening… no, I don’t think that’s really me. I just get stuck thinking about things more than I should, but not in a ritual kind of way.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're doing things in a specific order or way to feel more in control?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I like things done a certain way around the house, I guess, but I don’t know that it’s some specific order I have to follow to feel okay.\n\nLike, if I’m in the workshop, I’ll put tools back where they go, because otherwise I spend twenty minutes looking for a wrench I swear I just had. My father-in-law moved some stuff once—not even in a bad way, he was trying to help—and I got more irritated than I probably should’ve. But that’s more about wanting things where I can find them, not like I’m following a ritual.\n\nSo… no, I don’t think I’m doing things in a special order to feel in control. I just get bothered when things feel out of place, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel an overwhelming urge to perform specific actions in response to certain thoughts or situations?\n\nPatient: Overwhelming urge… I don’t think so. Not in the way you’re asking, I guess.\n\nLike, if something’s bothering me, I might go deal with it because it’s sitting in my head—like if I remember I left something in the yard, I’ll go check, because one time I left a tarp out and it turned into this whole mess after it rained. But that’s not like I have to do some exact action or else something terrible is going to happen.\n\nI mean, I get annoyed and I want to fix things, especially around the house, but I wouldn’t call it an overwhelming urge. More like… I don’t like things hanging over me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're consistently feeling down or depressed most of the day?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say most of the day, no… I mean, I have my stretches where I’m low or kind of worn down, but “depressed most of the day” sounds heavier than how I’d put it.\n\nSome days I’ll wake up already annoyed, like before anything’s even happened, and then I’ll go out to the garage thinking I’ll get something done, and three hours later I’ve mostly just moved things around. My neighbor once said retirement must be nice—though I’m not exactly retired—and I remember thinking, yeah, if only it felt like that.\n\nBut consistently down all day… I don’t know. I wouldn’t say that exactly. It comes and goes.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Yeah… yeah, it has. I mean, we’re still us, but there’s been a lot more tension than there used to be.

She gets frustrated because I’m not keeping up with things the way I used to. Like, I missed this contractor-license filing thing—important deadline, and I just… didn’t get it done. And then that messes with whether I can take certain little repair jobs, or I have to delay them, and then I’m at home acting like I’m busy but not really moving things forward. That puts pressure on her too.

And it’s not just work stuff. Family responsibilities, stuff around the house… I’ll say I’m going to handle something and then it sits there. She’ll remind me, and I get defensive, which doesn’t help. Half the time she’s not even nagging, she’s just trying to keep things from falling through the cracks.

So, yes, it’s changed. There’s more strain, more shortness between us. I don’t like admitting that, but it’s there.

It’s like everything around the house feels bigger than it is.\n\nEven when I go out to the workshop or try to fish, which used to be my way of settling down, my head just won’t quiet. So yeah… emotionally, it’s definitely affected me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to repeat certain behaviors or rituals to feel okay or to prevent something bad from happening?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like rituals, no. I mean, I’ll sometimes go back and check if I locked the shed or if I left a tool out, but that’s more because I’ve done that before and then the rain gets in—actually I lost a decent set of clamps that way once, which still annoys me.\n\nBut as far as having to repeat something to keep something bad from happening… no, I don’t think that’s really me. I just get stuck thinking about things more than I should, but not in a ritual kind of way.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're doing things in a specific order or way to feel more in control?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I like things done a certain way around the house, I guess, but I don’t know that it’s some specific order I have to follow to feel okay.\n\nLike, if I’m in the workshop, I’ll put tools back where they go, because otherwise I spend twenty minutes looking for a wrench I swear I just had. My father-in-law moved some stuff once—not even in a bad way, he was trying to help—and I got more irritated than I probably should’ve. But that’s more about wanting things where I can find them, not like I’m following a ritual.\n\nSo… no, I don’t think I’m doing things in a special order to feel in control. I just get bothered when things feel out of place, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel an overwhelming urge to perform specific actions in response to certain thoughts or situations?\n\nPatient: Overwhelming urge… I don’t think so. Not in the way you’re asking, I guess.\n\nLike, if something’s bothering me, I might go deal with it because it’s sitting in my head—like if I remember I left something in the yard, I’ll go check, because one time I left a tarp out and it turned into this whole mess after it rained. But that’s not like I have to do some exact action or else something terrible is going to happen.\n\nI mean, I get annoyed and I want to fix things, especially around the house, but I wouldn’t call it an overwhelming urge. More like… I don’t like things hanging over me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're consistently feeling down or depressed most of the day?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say most of the day, no… I mean, I have my stretches where I’m low or kind of worn down, but “depressed most of the day” sounds heavier than how I’d put it.\n\nSome days I’ll wake up already annoyed, like before anything’s even happened, and then I’ll go out to the garage thinking I’ll get something done, and three hours later I’ve mostly just moved things around. My neighbor once said retirement must be nice—though I’m not exactly retired—and I remember thinking, yeah, if only it felt like that.\n\nBut consistently down all day… I don’t know. I wouldn’t say that exactly. It comes and goes.\n\nDoctor: Has your relationship with your wife changed significantly since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, it has. I mean, we’re still us, but there’s been a lot more tension than there used to be.\n\nShe gets frustrated because I’m not keeping up with things the way I used to. Like, I missed this contractor-license filing thing—important deadline, and I just… didn’t get it done. And then that messes with whether I can take certain little repair jobs, or I have to delay them, and then I’m at home acting like I’m busy but not really moving things forward. That puts pressure on her too.\n\nAnd it’s not just work stuff. Family responsibilities, stuff around the house… I’ll say I’m going to handle something and then it sits there. She’ll remind me, and I get defensive, which doesn’t help. Half the time she’s not even nagging, she’s just trying to keep things from falling through the cracks.\n\nSo, yes, it’s changed. There’s more strain, more shortness between us. I don’t like admitting that, but it’s there.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
